The Missing Prompt","volume":"婚后外传：玻璃花房的香气比赛","anchor":"chapter-0208","paragraphs":["Cassian recognised Mara's perfume before seeing her. She was standing in the old glasshouse, wearing green silk and the expression of a woman about to steal something. \"Only a prize,\" she said when he asked.","\"Comparatively modest.\"","She touched the open collar of his shirt. \"I have the rest.\"","The municipality had lent the venue to a small perfume festival. A sign marked the watershed footpath above the venue. In the vicinity, a meadow was bright with flowers after a barren winter. A pasture lay behind the glasshouse, and growers from the region had brought flowers for demonstrations. Nell and Rhea, genetically close twins with utterly different tastes, were competing for the first time. Rhea was famed among friends for her bold mixtures. Nell formerly worked in a bookshop and was weak in the fashionable language of scent. She could remember a recipe perfectly if allowed to write it down.","The morning was disorganized, and Nell could not evade a cousin who kept asking if she was mad to compete. The scary thought had occurred to her already. Their guild-like training club liked oral demonstrations. Nell was reluctant to ask for a printed prompt, though she could carry a short sequence in her head when calm. Under pressure, she forgot words she used every day. Peer pressure was making a simple request feel like a confession. Mara asked for a copy of the instructions too, turning a solitary embarrassment into a mundane need shared by two people.","In many cases, a fancy presentation box concealed a tiny bottle. Nell wanted a coherent explanation to matter more. The print edition of the programme had given Nell the wrong surname. A thoughtful volunteer corrected it with a pen. The mistake did not change her origin, though a guest tried to trace it to North Africa from the sound alone. Nell had grown up in Yorkshire. The guest's cheerful determination to guess threatened to bewilder everyone. \"You can ask me,\" she said, with an unforced smile that cost more effort than it showed.","A Wall Street visitor predicted the expensive brands would dominate. Their investment returns, he said, proved customers knew quality. Nell's scores would be correlated with her confidence, he added. Rhea told him she was not sure what he was measuring. Price might be associated with a recognisable label rather than a better smell. The implicit argument interested Mara in relation to the price labels. She could convert a boast into a useful question by asking the judging panel to taste nothing with their eyes.","\"Smell,\" Cassian corrected softly.","\"I'm creative. Allow me latitude.\"","She could knock off a tasting slip more readily than name a flower. The tasting slips amused him enough that she kissed his cheek before he could deploy another joke.","With the exception of one damp aisle, the room was ready. His ladder was higher than the doorway. Ivo had volunteered to monitor the building. He described the glasshouse as a supersystem of ventilation, alarms and doors, then explained which part visitors actually needed to know: follow the staff if an alarm sounded. The enhanced display was meant to prevent confusion, not dominate decision-making. A functional switch was more useful than a colourful screen no one could interpret. He could cut down the noise by disabling the unnecessary sound effects.","Nearby, a science corner displayed a labelled picture of limbic structures among other brain anatomy. It made no promise that a bottle could control anybody's emotions. A promoter tried to refer to the perfume as a memory treatment anyway. Mara's thoughtful expression vanished. His repeated claim began to enrage the organiser, who put him on notice. He could describe a fragrance; he could not turn a vague reference into a medical claim. The message was chiefly plain common sense.","A visiting grower from South Carolina showed photographs from Australia, a trip across a hemisphere from her home in America. Another print showed a desert garden in North Africa, with a precise label rather than an assumption about the whole territory. Nell wanted to enter a scent dedicated to her grandmother. Rhea preferred the bright mixture she had developed alone. Neither was wrong. The dominating family habit was to call one twin sensible and the other talented. It could humiliate both in different ways. Genetically close did not mean socially interchangeable. Nell's devoted sister could support her without choosing her final note.","The cousins seemed to conspire to invade her concentration with advice. Nell had to exert patience in the midst of it. At the peak of the first demonstration, a trolley wheel struck a cable cover. A bottle tipped. Mara instinctively caught the tray while Cassian steadied the trolley. The bottle remained upright. The technician methodically checked the cover instead of blaming the nearest guest. An indirect risk had been averted before anything dramatic happened. Nell looked amazed; Mara had kept her hands on the tray rather than strike a victorious pose.","The weather was changing. Rain threatened the outdoor presentation, and a leak in one roof segment might force them to shorten the programme. Financially, cancelling the whole event would hurt the small makers. That could not justify ignoring a problem. They would deploy staff to the affected aisle and seek a middle ground: keep the sound area open, move the delicate displays. Economically, it was a smaller change than rebuilding the day from nothing.","Nell was on the verge of withdrawing. She did not want to go against the club's preference for a flawless demonstration. Mara suggested they break down the task into three ordinary steps. A prompt could be used to keep the sequence clear. A second sheet could be used to record quantities. Neither was redundant merely because somebody wanted the scene to look effortless.","A wholesome lunch had been prepared, though Mara could faintly smell something sweeter nearby. She would take the competition seriously once the samples arrived. The first rounds began. Cassian said he was weak in distinguishing flower names but strong in knowing when his wife wanted him to stop talking. She said his accuracy was improving. Then a sharp chime rang, and every door light in the glasshouse changed colour at once."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1033,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0208\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：玻璃花房的香气比赛</div><h1>1. The Room That Changed Its Mind","volume":"婚后外传：玻璃花房的香气比赛","anchor":"chapter-0209","paragraphs":["The weather was unpredictable. In the midst of a comparatively calm demonstration, the warning did not mean the glasshouse was on fire. Predictably, several guests reached for cameras before looking at the exit. Water had entered a detector housing, and Ivo was checking the affected circuit. Until staff confirmed the cause, visitors left the central aisle by the marked doors. Mara took Nell's tray; Cassian offered an arm to a guest who walked slowly. A steward helped avert a bottleneck in the vicinity of the porch. The twins, genetically similar and differently frightened, stood together beneath the covered porch.","At the peak of the confusion, a promoter announced that the perfumes had overwhelmed the building. The story was chiefly nonsense. It did, however, threaten to humiliate the makers and enrage the organiser. Nell's comparatively faint fragrance could not account for a faulty roof. A devoted volunteer would not be associated with that rumour merely because she had been nearest the spilled water. The account could bewilder people who had not seen the drip. The crew would check before blaming anyone.","Ivo referred to the building as a supersystem again while explaining the fault to a trainee. A problem in one component had sent a signal to several others. They could break down the sequence without pretending every part was broken. A clipboard was used to hold the instructions dry. His account was coherent. The alarm was scary; the repair was mundane. The trainee could carry the short sequence in his head after Ivo pointed to the actual devices.","The municipality's caretaker arrived from a nearby pasture, where he had been helping at another venue. The crew was dedicated to checking the roof before reopening. The region was wet, and the old roof had leaked before. Formerly, a bucket had caught the drip. An enhanced visitor installation had made the room look more sophisticated without repairing the source. The implicit assumption that improved appearance meant improved fabric had been mistaken. It was a comparatively inexpensive repair, but it had to happen before the affected area reopened.","Rhea wanted to go against the cancellation of the next round. She was on the verge of tears and mad that a whole year of effort might vanish. Nell could not evade her own disappointment either. The abandoned tables looked disorganized, but their samples were safe. The sisters were financially stretched and had paid for their entry. Economically, a refund would help. Emotionally, it would not replace the moment they had imagined. Mara heard both truths without offering to buy them a happier face.","The organiser proposed an outdoor table in the dry pavilion. With the exception of the demonstration rig, their supplies were portable. She could convert a cancelled round into a smaller live session. The creative solution would cut down the number of spectators, so each group could smell the samples properly. Nobody would invade another maker's table to get a better view. A steward would deploy the waiting guests in small groups, then let the next ones enter.","Peer pressure still troubled Nell. She was reluctant to stand at the front, and her sister instinctively tried to answer for her. Mara gently asked Nell what she needed in relation to the demonstration. A prompt, she said. She was weak in public speaking, not in recognising her own materials. The distinction had been clear since the beginning. A thoughtful sister could take the request seriously without treating it as a verdict on her future.","The display was colorful even without the electronic sign. The Wall Street visitor was waiting by a panel about the Yorkshire makers. A contributor from North Africa had supplied a separate note on one ingredient's origin. He was still sure that high scores would be correlated with the prices, an indirect way of insisting on his first guess. He began talking about investment returns again, then fell quiet when Rhea asked whether he liked a sample without its label. His reaction was deliciously uncertain. He could not carry a price list in his head and honestly call that the same as smelling.","Their guild-like club leader suggested a middle ground: the competitors could use notes if everyone did. Nell disliked the solution. She wanted permission, not a new obligation imposed on everybody. The leader reconsidered. He could refer to the notes as an optional aid, and the judges would assess the stated work. The print edition had not forbidden them. Socially, the restriction had grown through repeated expectations nobody had written down.","Ivo returned with the labelled diagram of limbic structures from the science table. It had been splashed and needed drying. The anatomy lesson was unrelated to the detector fault. He would not let a promoter conspire to join the two because the story sounded impressive. Genetically inherited traits, old memories and a pleasant perfume were separate subjects; no bottle here rewrote a mind. The organiser removed the misleading placard entirely.","Cassian looked at the new tables. \"I could knock off a charming speech about perseverance,\" he offered.","\"You could also fetch cups,\" Mara replied.","He returned with them and kissed the back of her hand. \"I prefer the second commission.\"","The unforced exchange made Nell laugh. Her famed sister did not have to be the dominating presence in every photograph. She could stand beside her. He could exert a little influence, but he could not choose the judges’ reactions. The upside of moving was that fewer people crowded Nell. The wind had dropped, the samples were functional and the hosts were under pressure but ready. Mara was amazed by the change and hoped nobody would enrage the already tired cook, who had saved a wholesome lunch for the crew. The second round would begin outside, higher than the wet floor and lower than anybody's first expectations."],"questions":[],"wordCount":969,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0209\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：玻璃花房的香气比赛</div><h1>2. A Scent of Her Own","volume":"婚后外传：玻璃花房的香气比赛","anchor":"chapter-0210","paragraphs":["Nell had been reluctant to ask for help since the beginning. Her birthplace was Yorkshire, her ambition modest: one clear explanation today. Nell began by reading the first sentence from her prompt. Her voice was comparatively quiet, but everyone at the table could hear. The notes did not bewilder the judges or ruin the scent. Rhea stood behind her with the spare bottles, looking fiercely devoted and trying not to interfere. Mara decided that this was socially difficult work being performed rather well.","At the peak of the little presentation, the wind lifted Nell's sheet. Cassian caught it before it reached the pasture fence. He handed it back without a flourish. He had formerly been willing to perform any rescue in a handsome manner. Married life had taught him that some assistance worked better when it did not become the dominating event. Mara rewarded him with a glance that made the plain kindness entirely worthwhile.","The high scores were not correlated with the prices on the brochure, the club leader observed. The judges were dedicated to listening. In many cases, they asked about an ingredient before deciding on a mark. The small result did not prove a grand theory. It did make the Wall Street guest laugh at his own certainty. Investment talk could wait. Nell's fragrance was associated with her grandmother's garden; she did not claim that everyone would recollect the same thing. She could refer to the scent as a tribute without pretending to offer a shared memory.","The guild-like club had found a middle ground that preserved the work. Makers could use whatever prompts helped them explain it, with the exception of a prepared sales speech read instead of the demonstration. The organiser would update the print edition next year so the expectation stayed clear. A comparatively mundane correction could avert the same distress. It did not require changing Nell into her more confident sister.","This first unforced presentation was a watershed for her. Nell had carried the recipe in her head while practising. She was weak in recalling it under a room's attention. Peer pressure had made her reluctant to admit that difference. Now she could break down the sequence in a coherent way, chiefly because the prompt gave her a place to return. The creative work was hers, genetically related to no grand talent merely because her twin had a popular reputation.","The municipality caretaker had isolated the damaged part of the glasshouse. The implicit promise was that repairs would be done. A thoughtful sign explained the closure in relation to the leak. The supersystem diagram helped a trainee see how the warning travelled between components; it was not an invitation for visitors to repair the alarm themselves. Ivo would deploy the next inspection team after the crowd left. Financially, the roof needed attention. Economically, ignoring it would produce more cancelled events, not save a season.","Mara walked with Cassian into the quieter garden. He had tried to carry every flower name in his head and produced one that sounded like a railway station. She laughed until he threatened to knock off the whole lesson and kiss her instead. The proposal was not scary. She instinctively moved closer, giving her answer without words. His palm warmed the curve of her back beneath the green silk.","Nearby, the children were drawing the brain diagram. The teacher labelled the limbic structures without assigning a magic smell to each. An indirect reference in a perfume advert would not change anatomy into an advertising promise. The promoter had gone. Nobody would conspire to evade the correction by putting the same claim on a smaller card. The overdue correction was beneficial. The event could popularise a curiosity without selling a treatment.","With the exception of the interrupted session, the afternoon had gone well. The organisers cut down one repeated speech to make room for the missed demonstrations. The final discussion was cultural as well as commercial, and Mara enjoyed the smaller makers in particular. A famed designer stayed to hear the smaller makers. She did not ask them to invade one another's spaces or imitate her signature scent. She was on the verge of retirement and amused by the assumption that she must always be mad about competing with younger people.","Rhea won the panel prize. She found a spot for her ribbon on the table. Nell won a separate audience choice for the clearest presentation. Nell’s confidence was higher than before. The two colorful ribbons looked absurdly grand. The two results did not humiliate either sister. One recognised a mixture; the other recognised a way of sharing work. Genetically close people could enjoy different rewards without a family deciding which one proved more gifted. Their laughter was unforced when they held the ribbons together.","The organiser had to exert her voice to gather the last group. Formerly, she would have treated a louder announcement as the only answer. He methodically checked the last lead; the redundant speaker stayed off. Ivo offered the functional microphone instead. The enhanced signal let her speak calmly. It was a small reminder that effort and help could work together. One did not have to go against the other. The closing thanks were chiefly brief, and therefore more readily heard.","A stallholder from North Africa packed the origin cards while her friend from Yorkshire collected empty bottles. The venue's caretaker waved them toward the right exit; no one would be asked to walk through a working zone. Nell thanked the municipality volunteers who had converted their disorganized afternoon into something she wanted to remember. The region's growers had already begun to exchange plans for next year. The change was deliciously ordinary. The upside of a flawed day was that people now knew whom to call.","Predictably, Cassian wanted the last word about roses. On the drive home, Cassian asked which scent she had chosen. She had put him on notice about making more flower jokes. Mara touched his wrist. \"The one that reminds me of this.\"","\"Dangerously imprecise.\"","\"Thoughtful, though.\"","He smiled and kissed her fingers while the city lights rose beyond the glass."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1019,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0210\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：玻璃花房的香气比赛</div><h1>3. data-go=\"208\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"210\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x039c01","title":"1. The Villain Has Beautiful Hands","volume":"婚后外传：公爵与写错的新郎","anchor":"chapter-0211","paragraphs":["Cassian found his wife reading a novel whose villain looked suspiciously like him. The man had dark hair, a severe coat and an astounding talent for arriving precisely when the heroine needed a locked door opened. The serial had readers around the world. Mara was way ahead of her husband and at a particularly enjoyable chapter. She refused to tell him whether the villain survived.","\"I am not wholly convinced by his ethics,\" Cassian said.","\"I find his incentive persuasive.\"","Their mutual friend Zara had written the book during a middle-aged reinvention of her career. Childless and tired of being called lonely, she had created an entire empire of people who wanted to be alone for half an hour. Its supreme ruler was an unfortunate man with nineteen ceremonial appointments a day. Mara's mania for the serial was partly literary and partly revenge against Cassian's appointment book.","They were travelling ashore for Zara's reading retreat. The ferry's atmospheric cabin offered ample room, a variety of cakes and a body of passengers pretending not to listen. Rowan began to gurn over the top of a newspaper. Cassian's amiable face gave away nothing until his wife read a line aloud about the villain's beautiful hands. His obsession with recovering the book grew visibly less abstract.","\"Abstractly, I approve of artistic freedom,\" he said.","\"Limitedly?\"","\"Very limitedly.\"","Zara had a lecture to give about invented worlds. Her geo-centric sky was deliberately fanciful, her case-ending system carefully designed, and the constitutionality of a fictional king's decree central to the plot. She could formulate a convincing legal problem without pretending the fantasy law applied here. A fine-grained note explained the grammar too. Her invented case-ending identified who received a gift; a careless edit had once made the heroine marry a table.","The retreat aimed to improve the lot of young carers through its proceeds. Noor had supplied a sober note about under-nutrition in one community project. Mara could go along with the fundraiser and remain biased about the book. The event did not offer biomedical treatment, and the anecdotes were not peer review. It offered an enjoyable afternoon and an agreed donation. The publisher had negotiated a thirty-day credit term with the printer, allowing copies to arrive before ticket money cleared. The local economic boom had not made every small press rich.","On-line ads now promised a very different event: a private meeting with Zara in return for an extra fee. She had approved no such offer. A hijacked media page was spreading a made-up programme. The story would invariably reach people before the correction if they sat debating it. Mara put down the book. This was no longer a cute annoyance about a fictional husband.","Anyhow, panic could not take up the whole afternoon. The false notice had threatened to unhinge Zara. They needed to refocus. The organiser would not withhold the accurate programme. A mild breeze allowed the cabin’s scent to diffuse. A loose connection could knock out the projector, so the crew had checked it before opening. The real event comprised a reading, a short workshop and tea. Staff were accountable for that programme, not for a stranger's promise. A cohesive response would be more useful than rushing back and forth in a panic. Mara asked the organiser to prioritize an ordinary notice at the door while the platform team secured the account. Cassian contacted the venue and gave the details convincingly. The account was similar to the real page, but its links were different. Those differences would bear on the response. Indirectly, the false promise could agitate guests enough to spoil the whole day.","Zara was acutely embarrassed. \"I should have noticed.\"","\"You did not post it,\" Mara said. \"You can be associated with a book without being responsible for every use of its title.\"","The distinction might exert an influence on her conscience later. For now, she was enraged. A sceptical young writer had already called the entire fundraiser a fraud. She was currently a little unprepared for confrontation. The account looked similar to the real one, and the fake banner bore Zara's photograph. Anecdotal screenshots were useful, and an invisible technical fault might explain nothing. It was all too easy to generalize from that. The venue would not disparage the mistaken visitors. Their suspicion had some degree of explanation.","Rowan suggested they hunt down the false host, who was supposedly waiting at a dockside café. Mara had no intention of confronting a stranger alone. She would meet Cassian there with the organiser and ask about the offer in public. The predominant mood at the table was combative. They could break up the gathering after a clear explanation, but not before they knew whether paid guests were already waiting.","An abnormal start could still lead to a good day. The retreat handout asked what a storyteller might imagine thousands of years hence. A visitor had scribbled beneath it: \"A less annoying registration form.\" Zara laughed despite herself. Her complaint about alphabetism had begun with always being last on a school register. Zara could deduce that her name caused the position without calling the system sinister. The same alphabetical sorting had placed her at the bottom of the signing schedule. She was doubtful that the universe held any malice. The queue certainly did.","A middle-class visitor offered to help carry the signs. His kindness was instrumental in making space. The man at the café was wearing an expensive badge and a wholly unconvincing smile. Mara's appearance seemed to depress him. Cassian had never seen anyone look so disappointed by a beautiful woman walking toward him. His wife was not in need of rescuing; she was in need of room to ask the first question.","\"Can I join your private reception?\" she asked.","The man said yes. \"You are entitled to a personal reading.\"","She would not overrate a badge. The host watched his confidence evaporate. Mara smiled. \"How nice.
